ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e337	In several areas, there is an overall committee which supports government policy in the legislative part of the revised National Budget for 2015. This means unanimity related to legislative changes such as: exceptional provision in the land interest rate tax on self-production in industry. This applies to the deduction of payments to non-resident pension schemes. It also applies to the extended possibility of providing regulations on intra-group transfers across borders. It is agreed that the oil tax regime will be removed. There is also agreement on a new regulatory framework requiring cash registers. Furthermore, with the exception of the Centre Party, there is also an agreement on new depreciation rules for wind power, which can be an important contribution to increasing the new renewable production of electricity in Norway. Then there are some important changes in the law that a majority backs up, which relate to transfers of tasks from the customs and tax haven to the tax haven. This is part of a major reform undertaken by the government to improve tax management, by bringing together tax and tax management into the tax system. The reform also provides that customs’ core tasks related to the movement of goods and border controls should be strengthened. The tax revenue will be subject to overall control responsibilities in the field of taxation, including taxes on goods, which together will strengthen control in this area. These changes are part of the government’s efforts to achieve better and more efficient use of resources in general government, and these changes will also contribute to improving the provision of services to citizens. I'm assuming that the minority will argue for their views on these questions. It also concerns the issue of the governmentisation of the chemical function, which is perhaps the area that has received the most attention in the context of the national budget this spring. Various organisations, municipalities, regions and individuals have had a high level of involvement in this matter. In the view of the government and the Right, the government will make an important contribution to strengthening the control of employers and thus the fight against labour-life crime and the black economy, and in the government's proposal there was also a proposal to strengthen this effort with 59 annual works from 1 January 2016. We also see it as an important contribution to equal treatment and greater legal certainty, and not least it should also be a simplification measure for taxpayers, which does not relate to several agencies, such as today. Through the examination of this matter in the context of the revised national budget, a majority have noted that there are some issues that can be further elaborated on, among others. the transfer of business and the rights of employees in that connection and the consequences for municipalities of a change. Furthermore, it is natural that the proposed changes can also be seen in a wider context with other ongoing change processes. This implies that the majority does not support this amendment from 1 January 2016 but asks the government to further address these issues. A minority promote proposals for extended country-by-country reporting. There is broad agreement on the importance of focusing on issues related to capital volatility and transparency on cross-border capital flows. The government is also very concerned about finding good solutions to combat this, and Norway is already a priority country when we have implemented and put into force national country-by-country reporting requirements to make the activities of businesses visible and to avoid companies draining poor countries for non-renewable natural resources. We are already going beyond the EU rules in this area. The majority do not support this proposal because an evaluation of the Norwegian country-by-country legislation has already been announced after three years and the EU itself will evaluate its legislation in 2018. In addition, these are global issues that are addressed by several countries – because it is important in the fight against poverty, but also because it is important to protect our own tax base in Norway as well as in other countries with which we can compare. Therefore, there is already a great deal of focus on so-called tax havens in many Western countries, and therefore international cooperation is also through, inter alia, body Financial Action Task Force important. Finally, I would like to thank the members of the Committee for their good cooperation in this matter.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e412	First to the representative Irene Johansen: I guess that's to say that the real tax and tax debate is now on Friday. The position we are discussing today on changes in tax, tax and customs legislation shows a high degree of political agreement on most of the amendments proposed by the government, and the Committee endorses the necessary corrections in order to maximise the effectiveness of the decision of the Storting in the 2015 budget in particular. EEA legislation and requirements for ESA notification. The results of the previous Stortingen’s decision on the reform of the malpractice have received considerable public attention since its introduction on 1 January of this year, due to the transition to gross benefits and new tax rules. The transitional mechanism proposed by the Right-wing Party Government in the 2015 budget for grossing-up with large interest-rate deductions is now further reinforced by a change in the limit of compensation in the event of a decrease in income from NOK 6000 to NOK 4000. This means that even more are covered by the scheme and those already covered receive a larger tax deduction. The three-year transitional system provides this group with insufficient time to adapt to a new income situation and make use of the possibilities to combine work and social security for future income hedges. In the context of the debate and decision on a new regime in 2011, the Progress Party was concerned that the government should focus on the possible negative outcome of the comprehensive reform, and it is therefore satisfactory that the Right-wing Party Government should follow this up and make adjustments to ensure a smooth transition to a new social security system. Reforms are difficult – not to propose, but to implement. The Norwegian economy is now characterised by the need for conversion, and successful private sector conversion requires that there is also a willingness and ability to convert into the public sector. Therefore, the government’s proposals for tax management reforms are important contributions to improving resource use, coordinating the activities of public agencies, red tape and improving efficiency. During a transition period, it becomes particularly important for government and growth to take full responsibility for the fact that the organisation of government is not static and tied up in traditional thinking but dynamic and proactive to meet new challenges arising in a constantly changing society. The government has proposed a number of organisational changes to improve tax management with a focus on simplification, non-discrimination, legal certainty and coordinated action against financial crime. By transferring collection tasks from the duty and levy rate to the tax rate, the tax and tax administration is brought together in a single unit and the customs core tasks related to the movement of goods and border control are strengthened. This is part of a major reform aimed at building a good environment of expertise and exploiting existing IT systems in simplification and red tape, for the benefit of users. An important consequence of the changes is simplified charging for importers. The government’s proposal to transfer the tax collection function from municipalities to the tax administration involves a comprehensive and important reform – not only because of efficiency gains, but in order to strengthen the control of employers and the fight against crime at work. Opponents of the reform have pointed out that current tax collection is effective, and there is no need to make changes. The starting point for the reform is not that local authorities are not doing a good job today. The tax collection procedures are ensured by common IT systems and national tax accounts established in the tax haven. 99.7 pst. the advance payments are made by uninvited employers, and the government's proposal for a new organisation is primarily to strengthen the control of employers and to establish a more effective control system to detect financial crime and thereby allow for the extension of the tax base. The many hearing opinions of different bodies confirm the need for changes and support the reform, with the exception of those carrying out the task today. The structural changes proposed concern different levels of administration and have implications for employees and municipalities as employers. The majority of the Committee would like these issues to be examined and seen in the context of other reforms and structuring of the tax rate. That is why the government's proposals are not being adopted at this time.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e483	In 2013, the majority endorsed the regulation on country-by-country reporting. The Regulation lays down rules on country-by-country reporting for undertakings in the extractive industry and forestry activities in non-planted forests. The reporting shall include information on payments to governments and other activity-related information and shall contribute to the economic activities of undertakings. The reporting rules introduced a few years ago contributed to greater transparency in the activities of the extractive industries. It is an industry with major global challenges when it comes to transparency and combating corruption and the deterrence of the values of poor countries. The proposers at the time were very concerned that the law could work, that it could be as useful as possible, and that it could create the least possible costs for the companies – although the last consideration is secondary in this context. Since that time it has not happened that much. Norwegian civil society organisations have contributed significant knowledge, which has highlighted both the case and the process. Over the last few years, a number of media projections have revealed large social costs in an inadequate reporting law. The regulation put forward in due course by the Ministry of Finance was an important step towards reducing the social costs of lack of transparency. At the same time, however, it has been pointed out by several actors that the regulations we have today have holes that will prevent effective compliance with the purpose of this legislation, namely to combat illegal capital movements, tax evasion, fraud from the people of poor countries. According to the Publish What You Pay Norway organisation, there are actually only a few small adjustments left before country-by-country reporting can ensure satisfactory reporting. Firstly, all countries included in the financial reporting of upstream results must be included in the regulation. So this applies to all countries that the companies themselves have defined to be within their upstream activities. Section 3 of the Regulation states that the report shall be: «distributed to individual countries in which the enterprise operates in the extractive industries». Uncertainty has been linked to what ‘activities’ actually mean. It opens e.g. doubts as to whether only producer countries or all the countries in the income and cost chain related to the activity are included. Secondly, it must be specifically stated in the legislation that group reporting is in line with the accounting figures for upstream activities. Thirdly, the full accounting context of the country-by-country reporting must be included as a note to the annual accounts rather than a separate reporting. This may sound very technical and boring, but it's important. It has already been done in France, which has already imported EU rules in the field. It also includes country-by-country reporting requirements for banks, and that the information must be provided in notes to the annual accounts, country by country, not consolidated. Since the country-by-country reporting requirement will also apply to banks in Norway in the long term, a level playing field should be created. It's also the cheapest way to report. Publish What You Pay Norway – among others – argues that it is fully possible to produce not very extensive note information. Fourthly, there are some unfortunate exceptions that need to be removed. The regulation today provides for the possibility of circumvention by the fact that a subsidiary may be excluded from the layout. It's very unfortunate in a world where tax havens cause thousands of billions of dollars to be stuck away from ordinary taxation and sharing with the community. Today, we have the opportunity to adopt improved country-by-country reporting legislation. It is not going to be adopted, because – as we have heard from the Left – it is that the cooperation parties have entered into a binding agreement with the government not to go into other comments or proposals. That means we have a week to find a way to promote this again – on Friday – that allows the cooperation parties to vote for. We are in a rather strange parliamentary situation when the government parties vote against each other in ‘hut and rifles’ on matters of pensions, on issues of Syrian refugees, which have considerable budgetary implications, while opposition parties are forced into the ranks of the government. In this case, it is unacceptable. It may defecate as a technicality and a somewhat complicated matter for particularly interested people, but it is Norway's contribution to a world where it is not such that huge values are passed away from those who were to make good on these revenues, at first. I would like to encourage the Left and Christian People's Party to think again and provide a majority for extended country-by-country reporting.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e636	The responsibility for tax collection is now divided between the tax administration, which has the professional responsibility, and the municipalities, which have the administrative responsibility. The reason for the proposal to transfer the municipal tax collection to the tax bill is to get a better and more efficient tax administration. Such coordination will lead to simplification for taxpayers and businesses, and one will achieve a more holistic and effective task-solving. The most important argument, in my opinion, is that this will lead to a strengthening of the fight against black labour and the black economy. There is too little priority today, and bringing together the tasks and responsibilities of a single establishment will strengthen and coordinate the control of employers. There are 288 municipal tax collectors' offices today, and many of these offices have very small and vulnerable trade unions. Tax collection is not a natural municipal task where local political conditions have an impact, but a regulatory task that is to a small extent influenced by understanding. It should not be such that there is a large difference between municipalities when it comes to tax collection. Therefore, taxpayers will be given greater legal certainty and more equal treatment in this proposal. So we register, through the listening round, that the resistance to the proposal has been great from the municipalities; there are only 25 municipalities that support the proposal. On the other hand, the vast majority of the colleges have given positive hearing opinions. On 19 May, the Financial Committee held a hearing on the revised State budget, and in that context, the Municipality of KS Oslo argued and set an example of the continued task of tax collection in the municipalities. The Financial Agency in the Municipality of Oslo pointed out and spoke warmly about the importance of the competent and multidisciplinary professional environment. My reflection in that context was that this was a very good contribution to transferring the tax collection from the municipality to the tax administration. I mean the municipality of Oslo is not representative of the 288 chemical offices, but that they with their many years of work and their strong professional environment, etc. can operate an active control business against the black economy. We see that the proposal to regulate the tax collection function from 1 January 2016 does not have multiples in the committee, but I am pleased with the comment from the majority in the committee, which sees that there are issues related to business transfer and adding their rights that need to be elaborated more closely, and that it would be natural for such a major change to be seen in conjunction with others. other reforms in progress and the division of tasks between State and municipality.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e825	In the last few months there has been an exciting and robust process in the nutrition committee. This has been the job of ensuring a broad political majority for the framework conditions of the aquaculture industry. The farming industry is a very important industry for Norway. Many point to it as one of the industries that will take over after the oil. The Economic and Social Committee wishes to establish long-standing policies, a policy which is pursued independently of the parties in the government offices. This is done only through broad political conciliation. The result is also something as rare as an overall nutrition committee. I would like to thank my colleagues in the committee very much. They have shown willingness to cooperate and the ability to find good solutions, and they have had a constructive approach to work. I'm fascinated by success stories – stories about someone thinking new, that you've had the courage to bet, people who've served both the country, the community and themselves. I'm impressed by those who have the ability to see new solutions and create new jobs in new industries. It's the story behind one of Norway's biggest adventures, the history of the aquaculture industry. Yes, we have natural assumptions that are unique in the world. The Gulf Stream gives us perfect flows and water temperatures to operate in aquaculture. The aquaculture industry was before a reintroduction for some particularly interested parties. Today it is a multi-billionaire company that exports values for over 45 billion. kr every one year. This shifts the aquaculture industry completely to the front with oil and gas and maritime industry. The marine industry is a very important export industry for Norway. In addition, aquaculture is very important to our coastal communities. All along the coast there are farming facilities that create both jobs and income for municipalities. There is room for enormous growth in the aquaculture industry. Probably we can double the output by 2050. But growth must be sustainable. Environmental challenges have been and are still major. Escape means that the farmed salmon threatens the wild axis of the rivers. Many cases of salmontus both damage the fish in the farms and kill the wild fish. In addition, emissions from installations, which are currently not a major problem, can become a problem if production is to be fivefold. The Committee is quite clear on the following: the environment should premise the farming industry, not vice versa. That's why we're in favour of a zero vision both in terms of escape, and in terms of resistance to salmonnus. A strategy on escape and an action plan on resistance to salmonids shall be developed. There is no doubt that the farming industry has an environmental impact. The environmental impact of each installation is zero. The overall environmental impact in the new production areas is weak, but no longer questions whether the farming industry has an environmental impact. That environmental image has to be limited. Therefore, the Committee considers it irresponsible to facilitate a new growth regime now. First, we have to have good enough models to establish the responsibility of farms for mortality on wild fish. It is important from a sustainability perspective. Therefore, the Committee asks for more knowledge about the impact of the farming industry on wild fish. We want to make sure that it's the environment that's making the premise. Then the models have to be safer than today. So far, we are proposing that the world's most stringent environmental legislation should continue. The government must be able to produce growth, but according to very strict criteria, in particular as regards the environmental impact of the industry. The position we are discussing now also takes care of small and medium-sized players. The government wanted the price to be paid for growth by the actors to be auctioned between the actors. The problem with this was that small players might not have as much capital as large, and that actors who spent a lot of capital and resources fighting lice and escape did not have as much capital left to win auctions. With the auctioning principle, it could be risked that it was only the few large ones or those who had not cared so much about lice control and escape, who eventually got the growth. There would have been an unwise regime when we try to get rid of the problems of lice and escape and also want more small and medium-sized players to remain in the industry. The Committee has therefore proposed a new concession pricing regime. New concessions determine the price through a combination of auction and fixed prices, while the fixed price principle is applied to growth on existing concessions. The municipalities will also benefit from the work of the Business Committee. In the past, the municipalities have received 40 pst. of the price of new concessions and 50 pst. of the price of growth. The rest has gone to the state. The Business Committee has now proposed that municipalities and county municipalities receive 80 pst. of both allocations. This will mean that municipalities and county councils with breeding activities will receive about 400 million. kr more every two years if two out of five farms qualify for growth. You have to qualify for growth, and that's the lusa that decides. The Committee also proposes that municipalities which have been raised today receive funds from the Marine Breeding Fund. For me, it is important once again to stress that in the setting we now have for treatment, we state that it is the environment that should be a premiscuous supplier. That is why I was surprised at the response of two of the parties at Stortinget who themselves believe they are the best on the environment. The Greens reacted to the fact that we were slowing down the government's growth plans. The Greens thought the time was overripe for growth. Obviously, it was not so important for the professional community to first develop good models for the environmental impact of the industry. The professional environment is uncertain about the proposed model. It was made clear through the hearings. They disagree if it's good enough. Therefore, the Trade Committee believes that it is a good idea to wait with a new growth regime. The models to measure the environmental impact must first be good enough. But obviously the Greens believed that the trade unions here are wrong. They thought it was possible to adopt 6 pst. growth every two years already now. I have to say it surprises me, since a few months ago we discussed a proposal from the Greens where they proposed halting the growth of the farming industry. The SV bombed as much in its criticism as we had our press conference. The main criticism came from the representative Knag Fylkesnes. According to him, the committee opposed the government's wish to introduce salmonmus as an indicator. He said the following to IntraFish: "It's amazing. Everyone has asked for sustainability indicators. Then you end up with one sustainability indicator -- salmonnus -- and then even it gets sent back. There is quite a lot of research into this.’ This is simply an error. In the vote, a joint committee shall state the following: ‘The Committee supports the initial lice level as an environmental indicator for growth.’ Furthermore, the Committee asks that emissions should also be identified as an environmental indicator, and all, except for the Progress Party, ask for indicators on waste and disease to be investigated. It's not just salmon-puss that's going to be an indicator. Disappearance, disease and emissions shall also be identified as indicators. The most important thing for the Christian People's Party has been that we can be sure that future growth in industry is sustainable. The environmental impact must be minimised. I believe that the government has been given good tools in further efforts to facilitate a good growth regime – a growth regime that sets the environment and biodiversity as a premiscible supplier. With these frameworks, the farming industry can have a fairytale future in our country. I recommend with the attitude of this committee.

Here I think there is a challenge in the knowledge base of the Marine Research Institute, on which the message is largely based. The importance of nutrition for other marine species and other coastal industries is to a small extent taken into account. That's why we're proposing a knowledge acquisition about that. So we think that the time is ripe for the ban on chitin inhibitors and a strong reduction in the use of hydrogen peroxide. Kitin inhibitors have been illegal in use, but are increasingly used. It has demonstrated effects on shellfish and crustaceans and possibly other marine species, and thus other coastal industries. Hydrogen peroxide is a fantastic and natural remedy, but it's too much used, as we see today, and it's all of a sudden damaging both in mares and for fish. This is not a discussion for or against the farming industry. I have deep faith in the farming industry as a concept and as a way of life in the future. But as a good friend of the farming industry, I also allowed myself to be deeply critical of the state she is today. If we're really going to be able to become more people on this Earth and at the same time take care of nature, a viable and efficient aquaculture industry is one of the keys. We're not there yet. According to figures from the Statistical Central Bureau, the farming industry currently uses only less than 1 pst. of turnover in research and development — this in an industry with a very large surplus. Compared to other industries, the farming industry is defined as a ‘low tech’ industry. Should we really take the industry into a new division, resilient and efficient, where the brunts of the future are both near the coast and far at sea, both deep and shallow, always safe, always top quality – happy salmon – always bearer power, we cannot escape the fact that both we as public authority and the industry themselves need to use separation more in research, development and technology than we do today. Not to increase the bet I think Norway as a nation, Norwegian nature and the farming industry themselves are losing weight. Although some technological innovations have occurred before farming, the basic technologies are the same as when the industry started at Hitra in the late 1960s. As an illustration, there is still an open note that frame the fish, and nuts that often have to be replaced and that are vulnerable to increasingly uncertain weather. Basic technology is still the same. What if we bring together the world-leading technology environment in the oil industry to help develop future farming technologies? Competence and technology transfer from e.g. offshore is very exciting, and in March Norsk Industrie, Norsk Oil and Gas, Abelia and Tekna took the initiative for Transferit, which is just about it. SV is the only party, I see now, who has proposed it in connection with the revised budget, which has followed up this initiative. It would be exciting, it would really be a conversion of Norway. Of other areas that could lead us forward, Finnfjord can be called melting plants and their projects on algae development to develop feed. What if we were self-sufficient in feed for the farming industry in Norway based on the use of CO2 from e.g. melting and use of Barent Sea algae? It would be a beautiful vision, which I'll tip off the Center Party, which is so engrossed in food security, would be agreeable. Finally, only a few words about local ownership and gain. I think it is problematic that the committee has landed on having an area tax – if we can call it it – that only links to growth. There was a multiple for an area charge in this hall. Technological developments mean that there will be fewer jobs in breeding. We also see that there are fewer actors. Most of the values are actually taken from communities along the coast. Then it's only fair and reasonable that part of the turnover that happens there goes to the municipalities. In any case, I hope we will return to the proposal for a land tax later on, and that there is still a number in this room that is willing to go further with it.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e1068	Like all other parties in Stortinget, the Green Party considers the farming industry to be an industry with great potential for the future, not only within the industry’s current production but also as a basis for exploiting the species, the environment and the opportunities that lie in Norway’s fabulous port. But the premise that this is at all conceivable is that this industry must be sustainable, and sustainable in the sense that the word actually has to be interpreted, that is. it must be an industry which does not adversely affect the environment around it. And so recently, there was probably a status report from the Scientific Council for Salmon Management, and it says: ‘Spaced farmed salmon and salmon sauce generally appear to be the two unstabilised threats to the Norwegian wild salmon population. The problem with salmonids appears to be increasing in prevalence and there is a risk that salmonid infective pressures may decrease salmonid in several areas in the coming years.’ As is well known, salmon insight in Norway has been more than halved from 1983 to 2014, and the only reason we do not see a direct collapse in the wild salmon population is that the tax has been greatly reduced. This may not result from anything other than the way in which Norwegian farming has been managed in the last 20, 25, 30 years. There is the absence of measures, the absence of effective policies and the absence of requirements allowing the farming industry to continue to grow violently, although these problems are thus once again pointed out to be increasing and out of control after fully up-to-date research. The Green Party has repeatedly praised the Minister for Fisheries to propose new measures and mechanisms that are better and more committed and potentially more effective than their predecessors. It is good that we finally discuss a regime in which pressures on the external environment are used as a basis for total production, so that it is basically possible to envisage a reduction in production as a result of non-compliance with environmental indicators. But the agreement that the Storting has now agreed on is unfortunately something else. It maintains once again the main problem of the industry, opening up to continued growth, which will eliminate environmental improvements. This is the strong growth in industry over many years, which is why various environmental measures have not had the effect on the external environment that they were supposed to have. For it is true that even if only the best installations, which keep the lice requirements, grow, the lice situation for the coastal environment is governed by all the other installations, which do not comply with the environmental requirements. That's what's one of the big problems. Therefore, the tidy of the nutrition committee would be to put a stop to growth, for example. three years, as the Green Party has proposed in the Storting and spend that time solving environmental problems once and for all, not on growth in production. So instead, we've got a deal that opens up some good opportunities, but it's going to figure out what to do, which is going to wait for plans for lice measures, escape measures, etc., and which refers to zero visions that have existed for years. Unfortunately, we have heard this before – it is not the same as concrete measures. And it's not only sad for the environment on the coast, it's also sad for the farming industry itself that Norwegian policies and Norwegian population do not manage to do the job that it owes it to the industry and that it owes it to the coastal environment, namely finally to take the measures that have been proposed for years to solve relatively simple problems. The Green Party today makes three proposals, firstly that the Storting asks the government to include escape and disappearance as concessional indicators. So today we're talking about a large number of people who want to develop a strategy for escape. What has actually happened in the last 20 years, while millions of salmon have escaped? Making escape an indicator directly linked to concession terms is a very obvious measure, which will have real effect. So you almost have to ask yourself if that's why it's not introduced. An indicator of disappearance is also necessary. Except for 20 pst. the salmon in current production dies in the mermaids. A number raise very serious questions about fishing welfare, and it shows the need for far more effective fishing welfare instruments than the farming industry is currently required. Secondly, we propose that the Stortinget ask the government to introduce requirements for individual labelling of farmed salmon. It's an ancient proposal. Individual labelling is a prerequisite for tracing and retrieving escaped salmon and for providing effective sanctions for escape. The Greens are pleased that the Committee brings forward positive environmental aspects of closed installations and that the Committee now removes the requirement for compensation for land-based farming. It's a big step forward in lifting up such a technology. But that is why, as our third proposal, we propose that an investment grant should be established for closed installations or technologies with similar environmental benefits. Time has to be overdue to develop solutions that, from the start, remove the problems of escape and lice that are obviously integrated into traditional meridian technology, not only in the interests of the environment, but also in the interests of a breeding industry that needs to get a kick where kick works, to become a world leader in technology. It is very special to note that it has been a very long time since the last Storting discussed the environmental state of the other Norwegian industry, which has potentially emitted a fraction of something that is environmentally harmful. But we are not discussing this, because we have taken measures for the longest time, which, apart from automatically keeping the industry within the environmental requirements adopted by the Stortinge. But there is every reason to believe that we are going to meet in a short time and discuss again the environmental problems of the farming industry, because it is still not willing to take the concrete measures that we know will get this industry to solve its environmental problems. It's not doing industry a service, it's not doing the environment a service, and it's not doing the green shift a service. So we encourage the government to drive more new revolutions in it despite all the positive things in the government's proposal, and get there as we know we need to, to measures that actually make the farming industry in Norway a sustainable industry.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e1110	Norwegian aquaculture has grown adventurously rapidly, from a reintroduction of the 1960s/1970s to today's billions industry employing thousands of people and producing well ten million healthy meals every day. Norwegian seafood is our main export item for oil and gas, but scientists have established that there is a potential to sixfold the value creation in the marine industries from 2010 to 2050. Thus, the greatest increase — almost a five-fold increase — must be able to come from farming. However, this presupposes addressing a number of challenges. The steps we propose in this Communication will help solve some of these challenges. Norwegian farming industry is not only a leader in the production of healthy seafood in clean waters. The knowledge and technology we have developed by building this industry is also a great export potential. In the Communication, we want to stimulate further technological development by removing the number limit and remuneration for land-based concessions. Closed marine installations could be provided with green light for growth regardless of the lice situation in the area. And we want to create a new tool in the form of development concessions that can realise new technologies, for example offshore production. I believe that the export of healthy Norwegian seafood will double in the years to come, but the technology and equipment industry too has a very bright future ahead. I am therefore pleased that a broad majority of the Business Committee agrees with the government that this industry needs predictable framework conditions and that a competitive Norwegian aquaculture industry demands that the framework conditions remain constant over time. An overall committee also agrees that growth in Norwegian salmon and trout tops is to be environmentally sustainable. Consideration is given to the environment to determine the growth rate of the farming industry, not the market. The government has proposed linking growth in Norwegian salmon and trout tops to an action rule based on a system of environmental indicators in production areas. This is a system that provides strong incentives to operate environmentally sustainable. We will stimulate economic operators to invest in technologies and operating modes that help keep the environmental footprint within acceptable limits. Today’s farm does not affect only its own neighbourhood. They affect each other, but also the environment in a larger area. Salmon and nutrient salts are distributed with water streams. Therefore, we cannot only control production growth according to operational requirements at the locality level. We also need to manage the industry on the basis of what is an acceptable overall impact of all installations in a larger geographical area. A prerequisite for such an approach is the existence of a geographical area in which environmental impacts can be measured. Division into production areas is a process I will now continue. This process will also involve relevant actors, so that both industry, municipalities and administrators can be confident that we will find boundaries between the different areas that take into account practicalities — within the framework of what is natural science sensible. Today’s flexibility with interregional biomass ceilings will be ensured in the new growth system. I note that a broad majority of the Business Committee supports the government on important points, such as the establishment of production areas and the establishment of boundaries for these areas in the light of scientific assessments. Furthermore, I note that there is a consensus on an important principle, namely that the actors in an area will be responsible for the overall burden on the production area. There is a broad consensus that salmonmus and escape are the main environmental challenges of the aquaculture industry in the short term, and there is a broad political consensus that salmonmus is suitable as a growth indicator. I'm glad. It's important here to generate new knowledge, but it's very important to make use of the knowledge that we already have. We want to use the available knowledge from several disciplines in the completion of a model to measure lice loads on wild populations. Until the system is implemented, the research environment will have optimised the model, in line with the wishes of the nutrition committee. I am therefore confident that the model will be a good tool to assess whether there is room for growth when we arrive at the end of 2016/2017. There is also agreement that escape is less suitable as an indicator of growth. But that doesn't mean we're leaving the null vision of escape. On the contrary, we are taking this challenge very seriously. By «Rule on joint responsibility for defrosting, etc.» by escaped farmed fish’, the Government has ensured an obliging agreement with the industry on the financing of the catchment of listed farmed fish. The agreement will be an important instrument for better control of the challenges of escape, but it does not mean that we are in target. Escape can still happen – as in the case of extreme weather in the last winter. The review by the Directorate of Fisheries of the many escape incidents has shown that mooring failures were the main cause of the hurricane’s severe consequences. I am now waiting for the report and conclusions of the Directorate and, if necessary, ready to launch new measures to prevent escape. Therefore, as the Committee asks, I would like to start work but a strategy against escape, including: the experience of the storm in the last winter will be included in the work. I am pleased that the Economic and Social Committee agrees that an area charge should not be imposed on the aquaculture industry. Municipality-Norway gets a lot left to be the hosts of the aquaculture industry. They receive jobs and tax income, and not just from the aquaculture industry itself. For each job in the aquaculture industry, four jobs are created in other establishments. It is government policy that municipalities should receive much of the remuneration for the award of growth. The Committee now proposes that a record proportion of the remuneration, 80 pst., will be allocated to the municipal sector. The Government, as requested by the Committee, will now start work on the creation of a Maritime Fund and develop allocation keys to ensure a fair distribution of the remuneration revenue, based on the ability and willingness of municipalities to be good promoters of the aquaculture industry. The Norwegian economy needs more growth industries. We need like never before more legs to stand on. There are good reasons why aquaculture should be one of the major industries we're going to live on in the future. Sustainable food production is perhaps one of the most sensible things we can use the Norwegian coast for. A predictable capacity adjustment system in Norwegian salmon farming will contribute to a bright future for an industry with a high growth potential. This forecast is a clear invitation for the farming industry to invest more in greener technology. The industry can effectively influence its own growth potential. A five-fold increase in farming by 2050 is both about making the industry greener where it is today, and about stimulating technological development, so that the industry can use new land both on land and beyond at sea over the next decades. In Norway, we have world-leading knowledge environments and ambitious business actors, and thus the very best prerequisites for success. Thus, small Norway can play an increasingly important role in delivering healthy food to the world.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e1361	At the request of the Business Committee, the President will propose that the speaking time be limited to 5 minutes to each party group and 5 minutes to the government. ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e1368	With regard to king crab management, it is an introduced species that has been commercially managed since the early 2000s in Norway. The management model is such that it has a commercial zone from the Russian border and east to North Cape. West of that border you have a disembarkation zone, or a zone where you're going to fish as much as you can. In many ways the administration of the king crab is a success story. One is concerned when a new species is introduced, this about how it should affect the areas, the biology and how to keep it under control. In the commercial zone, one has worked for 15 years with a brand project where there have been skilled fishermen and buyers who have found that they are selling the king crab – Norwegian king crab – exclusively, under the brand Varanger. This has meant that they have created significant values, and this has enabled us to build up a fleet of market competence in this area of fishing. In the past, they fished crabs, frozen them, and sold clusters, i.e. claws. So far, for a large part, you fish the crab, take it on land, and export it to the living. The paradox is that skilled practitioners fish the crab that comes walking from the Russian side, and deliver it back to Moscow for a little over 450 kroner per kilogram. It has to be called a good shop in the middle of it, which is really a bit of a sad story about an introduced species that shouldn't be there. The Communication goes on with the principles of administration that have been very much. One retains the two areas, but in order to professionalise the fishing a little bit more and fulfill what has been the intention both in the previous Royal Cancer Message and in this, the fishing and the values must go to those who have lost other fisheries when the crab came. You make a lot of demands. First, you set requirements for the size of a boat. Other fishing activities are also required in order to be allocated quota in royal crab fish. All of these have been well received in virtually all fishing environments, and there are interventions to promote a more professional and better fishing. You also change the quota year so that you can stretch the season and have available crabs all year round. It increases the value of consumers, and it increases the value of value creation. In this case, Mrs Sametinget has been consulted, and in those consultations there has been a great deal of agreement in most areas. It is largely just the discussion about the size of the boat that there has been a disagreement about. The argument from the government and in the message is that a boat over six meters makes you capable of caring for the crab in a proper way. There is also a safety aspect with regard to these fisheries. A crab seine is quite big to deal with, and you shouldn't be doing that kind of fishing with for small boats. There is broad agreement on everything in the committee. There is a minority proposal in a slightly special case. It has been discussed over a number of years whether fishermen in the municipality of Másøy can get a quota of their own. Mashøy is a border municipality of Porsanger, where the border goes. Thus, the municipality of Másøy is outside the commercial area and it has been discussed whether the Másøy fishermen can obtain a quota in the commercial area. Four-five years ago this was highly topical, because then the value of commercial fishing was so great that it actually meant a good deal to a fisherman who wanted to participate. The municipality of Másø experienced, among others: that people moved east from the municipality, took the boats with them and established themselves instead in East Finland to participate in this fishing. Things have changed today. First of all, there's a much larger disembarkation fish in the west. Secondly, you now have a purchase that makes you make the same amount of money on the crab you fish in the west as you earn on the one you fish in the east. So the big economic advantage of having an allowance in the commercial area is no longer there, and so the government says you have to have the zones and the breakdown that they've been. There's also a reason for controlling and managing the zone that makes it easier if you're absolutely sure you have a limit. That is why the Communication has said no to that, and the majority in the Committee also said no.
ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e1389	As the case-speaker did well, there is agreement that the model for the administration of royal crabs has our special success. One has actually managed to develop commercial fishing in the east with the two-part administration regime, while one has the ability to keep the stock under control of west deterrent fishing. The working party also agrees to introduce requirements for placing on the market in other fisheries in order to be allocated quota in royal crab fish, for which the coordinator also did well. In this context, the working party would also point out that some traditional fisheries are difficult to deal with because of the royal crab, e.g. fjord fishing for rays and flies - so that one to some extent may be adversely affected by the king crab even if one has not been caught. We also show that the difference between fishers on pages A and B with the relative half and the whole quota is over. Therefore, we ask that the upper and lower limit of the proposed model be carefully monitored and evaluated after some time. We have been aware of the importance of environmental monitoring, and we also point out that the system of tailings is still an important measure to limit the spread of royal crabs. There are two areas of intrusion in the Committee. The one is about those with boats less than six meters to get involved in crab fishing, the other about the fishermen in the municipality of Måsøy to participate. When we do not make any proposals for this, it has a clear connection with the fact that in the committee it is only the Labour Party and the Center Party that share this view. The presence of crabs, caused by the fishers from Måsøy, both by the fact that crabs may be a barrier to traditional fishing and indirectly through border problems against the commercial catchment area. It should be possible to create an arrangement where those who want to do so may seek to obtain allowances in the commercial area, but they will then be cut off from disembarking during the period they operate the commercial fishing – this to meet the control aspect. A boat that is looking to participate in the commercial fishing must give up the period to be fished, for example. January or February. Once the quota in the commercial fish has been set up, you can fish again in the demersal area. In the same way, it must be allowed for boats that have quota, and whether one is busied east of Nordkap – the border of the commercial area – to have disembarkation fishing driven after the commercial quota has been set up. It is desirable that most of the fishers in Måsøy should be fished with desimation. By providing them with access to commercial fishing, they will receive a safer income so that they can invest in crab fishing equipment. The working party is concerned that one must also include a mandatory and concrete design of activity requirements in the desimation fisheries, for example. in the form of supplied quantum disinfestation crabs, in order to obtain a quota in the commercial areas. I would like to stress from the Labour Party that it is important to consult the Sameting before any changes are made. I hereby accept the submission from the Labour Party and the Centre Party.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e1429	It has been an intention that king crab fishing in the quotad area of East Finland should be reserved for those most affected by by by-catch problems with king crabs in other fisheries, i.e. those which have partially destroyed their traditional fisheries and lost income after the entry of the king crab. We aim to simplify the process of granting tourist fishing permits to tourism businesses, so that we can also make the best use of the king crab in this industry.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e1606	The notification to the Stortinget on Norway’s fisheries agreements is usually widely agreed during the proceedings of the Economic and Social Committee. This was also the case this time when the Committee examined the notification by the Ministry of Industry and Fisheries of the fisheries agreements for 2015 and the fisheries implemented under the agreements in 2013 and 2014. It is an overall committee that is behind the position we are now discussing, and I would like to thank the Cooperation Committee for that. Norway splits up to 90pst. of the marine resources we harvest, with other countries. This means that it is very important to conclude good, forward-looking agreements. The Committee points out that the fisheries agreements are based on long-term administrative cooperation, which provides a basis for us today to reap from strong fish stocks and engage in economic activity in our marine areas and which also has a good impact on land. International cooperation is essential for successful and sustainable fisheries management and for combating, inter alia: fishing crime. The Committee also points out that Norway’s good reputation as a manager of large fish stocks gives us an advantage in other meetings and negotiations with nations with which we work. This position must be pursued by Norway in the future, and good resource control is an important element in this. There is reason to highlight Norway's work in international control cooperation, a cooperation between officials and industry, working together against illegal and unreported fishing. For my part, I would like to pay tribute to the Minister for Fisheries for the way it is worked. Another example of Norway’s good work is the participation of the Institute of Marine Research in the International Council for Marine Research, where the Institute of Marine Research contributes to both stock assessments and advice. Norway has significant scientific advantages and competences, contributing to a better use of resources — which in turn benefits both Norway and the countries with which Norway cooperates through different types of fisheries agreements. While it is important to have agreed agreements on populations we share with other countries, it is very important to be tough in order to safeguard Norwegian interests, and the Committee believes that the main focus is Norwegian interests. The relationship with Russia is challenging in various areas at the moment, and it is particularly positive that good, bilateral fisheries agreements have been negotiated with Russia in 2014 and now in 2015. With regard to the EU, the Committee has pointed out that Norway needs to continue cooperation with the EU with a view to the best management of the common stocks. Finally, a Joint Liaison Committee underlines the continuing need and importance of high priority for stock research. In such a priority, Norway will have a more precise basis for determining allowances and improving resource use. Increased stock research will also be able to generate growth and value added for industry in the future. I would therefore also like to point out that the State budget for 2015 was set at 10 million. extra to the Marine Research Institute to strengthen population research. An overall committee says that higher priorities are needed for stock research. Otherwise, reference is made to the setting.
ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e1637	Let me start by saying that I am pleased that the Committee is highlighting the importance of good resource control. Promoting the sustainable management of living marine resources is one of the three overall objectives underlying Norway's participation in international forums. I am all the more happy to inform Stortinget that we have put in place both a new landing and closing note regulation and a new registration scheme for the smallest fleet. The special notification system, which operates electronically, is a modern solution and one of several tools to meet the need for good resource control over the years to come. The Committee promotes international control cooperation, where we also achieve results. Norway and Russia agree to continue their control cooperation, including: on a Joint Analysis Group, but also with measures to reduce discards of fish and projects to contribute to common guidelines for the conduct of inspections. The International Conference on Fishing Crime, which we organised in Tromsø last autumn, has received very good feedback, including from international participants, and is something we are planning to repeat. The objectives that I mention here are all contributing to our being able to achieve one of the other of our overall objectives: to ensure satisfactory control and enforcement in fisheries. The work being done in regional fisheries organisations and in the United Nations has an impact on our fisheries management at home. I therefore support the Committee’s view on the importance of Norway’s participation in this work. NEAFC extended the scope of port State control in 2014. Port State control is now fully in line with the International Port State Agreement negotiated under the FAO, which is yet another step in the right direction in terms of international control work. Another effect of our international work is that in 2014, Norway was able to resume fishing for mackerel in Norwegian waters after many years. Good stock research is important, and it is uplifting that the Committee also wishes to give high priority to this. Despite the fact that the current stock situation is good for several species, we have monitoring and research challenges related to increased population prevalence and increased activity along our coast. The government therefore strengthened the Marine Research Institute’s efforts to handle marine data of 10 million. kr in the 2015 budget, and in the treatment, Stortinget further strengthened its efforts by 10 million. kr for stock research. I have also created a new advisory panel where the aim is to get good input from the industry on research and stock monitoring. In addition, the government is committed to ensuring that society gets the most out of the resources used for marine research and monitoring. The marine research institute is in the process of assessing the future needs of marine data collection infrastructure, i.e. vessels, buoys, etc. Even I'm working on a master plan for marine research. There we will further develop the marine research policy and specify the marine research priorities. Both of these works will be completed during the coming harvest. I started by saying something about one of the main objectives underlying Norway's participation in international negotiations. I want to round off with the third and last main objective – which is to ensure Norway a fair share in the quota regulation of common regulated stocks. After many and long negotiations we finally got a tripartite agreement on mackerel, which is very positive. We will continue to work to ensure that all coastal states adhere to the agreement in the long term. There are also some challenges that remain in the negotiations on coal and herring agreements, where our negotiators are working to achieve goals. In recent years, there has been a process to change the framework for the Skagerrak agreement so that it becomes more in line with modern marine law. This has now resulted in a new agreement on Norwegian, Danish and Swedish fishing in Skagerrak, which was signed on 15 January this year. For the first time, there is now agreement with the Russians to introduce quota flexibility on cod and hake. At the same time, for the first time, we have reached agreement with Russia on the common total quota and the allocation of ugar in the Barents Sea. Both are important milestones in our long-term cooperation with Russia. There can be no doubt whatsoever that it is important for us to conclude agreed agreements. At the same time, we cannot conclude agreements at any price – in addition to being good agreements for Norway as a fishing nation, they must also be sustainable.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e1690	Report. St. 25 for 2014–2015 On the right track, new tools are being put in place to achieve more coherent and efficient road development in our country. The three main elements of the notification to achieve this are the creation of a new road carrier, the framework for public–private cooperation and a toll reform which facilitates the transition from many to a few toll companies. Improved accessibility contributes to increased value creation and connects states and parts of countries in a new way. Good quality and good maintenance of roads are essential for both business and population throughout the country. The Committee is therefore concerned that more, better and safer roads need to be set up both in the districts and between regions and cities. It is interesting to recall that when the action rule was introduced in 2001, it was assumed that the increased scope of action provided for oil revenues would be used to strengthen the long-term growth capacity of the Norwegian economy, including: through infrastructure investments. For an overall committee, it is important to stress the need for greater predictability of development projects over time. Interruption is not very effective. The majority of the Committee, the Right, the Advance Party, the Christian People's Party and the Left, believe that a road reform as proposed by the government in the Communication will contribute to a more comprehensive road development with predictable funding. The same majority are pleased that a funding system is now in place in which planned and started projects can be implemented in the context. New funding opportunities and alternative forms of organisation create more predictability while increasing the rate of investment. Together, a newly created road carrier, predictable financing and use of OPS will be key elements in the renewal of Norwegian road policy. Because we are now seeing a renewal of Norwegian road policy, thanks to a new and offensive political majority. The red-green areas also recognize the need for change. But I am amazed that the country's largest opposition party has no new ideas to present for the future organisation of this sector. A Joint Committee is committed to stress that a good road network together with a well-developed and maintained railway is the very lifeblood of the terrestrial transport system. The carrier is proposed to be organised as a public limited-liability company. Development shall take place within the TEN-T road network, the largest European roads in Norway. The State Road Service shall continue to be responsible for the remainder of the national road network. It is through the roll-out of the NTP that the development portfolio of the company is determined. The majority of the Committee, the four cooperation parties, emphasise that the establishment of the road carrier facilitates cost-effective and rapid deployment. A more comprehensive and coherent development of the main road network is being achieved in Norway, while reducing planning time, providing predictable funding and establishing a road transport company with a pluralised profile in order to rig a more efficient European road development in Norway. The new strategic approaches help to strengthen value creation in our country and improve accessibility and safer roads for our citizens. Therefore, the establishment of the new road carrier provides added value in addition to traditional construction. The company’s portfolio is based on projects that were prioritised in NTP 2014-2023, with the addition of some intermediate road sections. In order to achieve a more comprehensive and coherent development, the majority consider it appropriate to include the intermediate road sections already at the start of the company. It is assumed that the portfolio will be gradually expanded and that the company can be given stronger financial muscles so that the company can gradually be given responsibility for several projects. It is important to stress that there must be consistency between the tasks entrusted to the company and the financial resources made available to the company. The road projects included in the start-up portfolio have been selected on professional premises and are at the same time a clear expression of taking seriously the objective of coherence and overallity. In order to achieve effective and targeted deployment, the majority support the company's expected end date for the start-up portfolio of 20 years after the company's establishment. Development will be financed through State resources and tolls. The company shall also be responsible for the operation and maintenance of the road it has built. The majority note that the government will continue to work on a comprehensive approach to the company's responsibilities for operation and maintenance, including funding. I record that the minority, the Workers' Party and the Centre, fear of professional fragmentation through the establishment of road companies and believe that the solution should be a separate project organisation within Vegvesenet, with commitments over several years for a selection of projects. Well, they almost have to go closer to this. The majority, the four cooperation parties, clearly state that the state road system will continue to be naved in Norwegian road development, responsible for the development of most roads to be built in the future. The carrier and the State Road Service will be two equivalent parties responsible for different road development tasks. It is important that one finds good border crossings, so that the total resources are used as best as possible. The majority are concerned that, through the establishment of the new road carrier, a good management of the human resources, which can ensure good competence both in the new company and in the state road system. Public-private cooperation, OPS, has proved to be a very useful form of development in terms of achieving a shorter extension period compared to the usual way. The criticism of OPS has been the way in which developers have had to finance the projects. Therefore, the majority are very satisfied that one is now adding to an adjusted OPS model by providing state guarantees for the expansion company’s borrowing. Using the country's creditworthiness lowers a borrowing rate. The majority support that the government considers using the same model for development also in the railway sector. The minority, the Working Party and the Centre Party express scepticism for the OPS model. The government is proposing a new toll reform aimed at reducing the number of toll companies. Furthermore, the issuer’s role must be separated purely organisationally and the government proposes the establishment of an interest-rate compensation scheme. An overall committee considers that there is an untapped potential for improving the efficiency of the toll sector and is concerned that a sheep for schemes that minimise the collection of car drivers’ payments is at the collection costs and is as feasible. The National Audit Office has also pointed out that there are economies of scale that have not been exploited well enough in the established toll management. The Committee supports the launch of efforts to create a system of fewer toll companies, a reorganisation that can contribute to increased professionalism in terms of collection and management. The Committee refers to the National Audit Office’s finding that the non-use of tolls has led to significant costs, which, according to the National Audit Office’s recommendation, should indicate that several road users are using tolls. The majority of the Committee, the Right, the Party of Progress and the Left, believe that toll bar should be optional, but nevertheless emphasise that there are administrative simplifications in the use of toll bar, which should provide different incentives for more drivers to make use of it. However, the majority believe that the requirement of mandatory toll bar would be detrimental to many. The minority, the Workers' Party, the Christian People's Party and the Centre, believe that the introduction of mandatory toll blocks for all vehicles above 3,5 tonnes is a step in the right direction, and believe that this should also be introduced for lighter vehicles to ensure that maximum tolls can be used. The majority, the Right, the Christian People's Party, the Advance Party and the Left, are also concerned with giving the piece extended use, which could be an incentive to get more people to put it into use. The same majority supports the separation of the issuer role from the toll companies in order to contribute to more efficient operation and lower costs. The Committee has noted that the government is proposing an interest-rate compensation scheme for toll loans for established and new toll projects forming part of one of the new toll companies. Toll projects are covered by national roads, county roads and municipal roads. The compensation is intended as a scheme designed as an annual grant for the projects concerned for up to 15 years. The Committee refers to the response of the Council of State, where it appears that there is uncertainty relating to the calculation of the amount of the annual amount of this scheme. A rough estimate for the national road network amounts to about 2016. 600 million. kr. The members of the Committee from the Right and the Advance Party are pleased that the government is committed to a toll reform that can contribute to the more efficient organisation and operation of the toll collection. These look at the interest-rate compensation scheme as an incentive for established toll companies to enter the new companies. The members of the Workers' Party and the Centre are sceptical about the interest-rate compensation scheme, as it will not contribute to the development of the road. The members of the Committee from the Christian People's Party and the Left do not take a position in principle on any interest-rate compensation scheme, as the appropriations for this purpose must be assessed in relation to the annual government budgets. I am pleased that the new bulk of the population has a completely different level of ambition for the transport sector, so that we can now bring about a comprehensive renewal of the sector. In 1962, Minister for Transport Bratteli presented the motorway plan, a plan for the construction of approx. 800 kilometers of four-field road, motorway, in South Norway by 1980. Similar plans were presented at the same time in Sweden. In Sweden the four fields were built, in Norway we built two fields. Safe and efficient roads contribute to growth that ensures the well-being of the future. It is investment in security and future value creation.

ParlaMint-NO_2015-06-15.ana.ud2166e1790	For the Workers' Party, road policy is one of the bones on which transport policy is based. Together with railways, maritime transport, public transport, corridors and bicycles, this constitutes the whole of it. Our national road network is the hearth of the national transport system that connects cities and parts of the country and connects Norway to neighbouring countries. This is part of the country's assets and a fully integrated part of a competitive business and employment. The relationship between mobility and economic development is not disputed. One thing is that the goods are going to the markets, but today, 87 pst is still happening. of the carriage of passengers by road. In our view, this transport is to be done with as few injuries as possible. The government has put forward a reform with something new and something old. The new is the road company and that the Progress Party is committed to the use of road tolls for the financing of roads and binds up the toll collection many decades ahead of time and over several collapse choices. The working party is concerned that the road sector is organised in such a way that the community gets the most out of the money. We want new and smart models to build a road, so we get more efficient road construction. We therefore look with a little wonder at the grip of establishing a new state road company. The proposal of the Right and Christian People's Party in the treatment of the National Transport Plan was to transform the entire state's roadway into a limited liability company. Now a company is to be set up on the side, which is to do exactly the same as the State Road Service already does, but on a limited selection of routes. In addition, they are not given the heavy authority tasks performed by the State Road Administration, nor are they required to address the major urban problems, as reported in the Communication. It provides increased administration and binds up more resources, resources that could instead be used to build more roads. We cannot see that there are any advantages in breaking up responsibility for the national road between two actors, nor can we see that there are good, professional arguments for this in the big news. The working party believes that there is a need to implement the development, operation and maintenance of the national road network even more effectively, but it is important to carry out proper analysis before making the changes. The government itself writes in the notice that the State Road Administration is an effective implementation organisation. Therefore, unlike the government, the Workers' Party would rather strengthen the State Road Service, which already has competence and experience in implementing large-scale road development. The Workers' Party will therefore ensure that the State Road Service can govern the development of national road projects in the same way as the proposed road company, so that it does not create a new, duplicated company, split up the professional environment and compete for limited road skills, engineers and planners. We believe that these are good arguments for more portfolio-driven progress on large projects, such as: on the free E39, the largest and most continuous road project in our time. Another element of the government’s road reform is greater commitment to public–private cooperation, referred to as OPS, in the field of road development. Traditionally, OPS contracts have been such that the State pays private companies over 20-30 years to finance, build and maintain public roads or construction. It has been described as a ‘heel in hat’ by a former State Secretary, so that the magnification representative again. Of course, the working party wishes to welcome new and smarter models for building and maintaining roads, so that we get more efficient development, but we are sceptical to traditional OPS deployment and believe that OPS projects are not a forward-looking, modern way of financing transport projects. The Labour Party believes that private debt financing of the road is poor management of the Community's money and that private financing of public road facilities provides greater financial costs, which ultimately have to be paid by the Community. We want new forms of contract that make it more possible for innovative solutions and better interaction between the state as a builder and the contractors, who are given the task of carrying out the investments. The third element of the government's road reform is a so-called toll reform. The Workers' Party supports the use of tolls, not because we want to plague people, but because there is a gap between those who use the road, and the authorities, which allows the community to expand more roads faster. The working party is concerned that it should be as effective as possible when we charge tolls, so that the drivers get the most way back for each crown they pay when passing the toll stations. We therefore welcome the government's commitment to measures to improve the efficiency and efficiency of the tolling sector and the follow-up of the decisions taken by the Government on this subject from the national transport plan. Here, the key elements for improving the efficiency of this sector are the merging of toll companies and the coordination of tariff and discount systems, which will reduce administrative costs and improve the efficiency of operation. That's very good. But the Workers' Party believes that here the government could have gone even further in improving the efficiency of the toll collection. Compulsory tolling is the measure itself which results in the greatest reduction in collection costs. We also read a small desperate attempt from the Progress Party to decorate the fact that they are in government for tolls and tie up the toll collection for many decades and generations to come with this message. The government is proposing an interest compensation scheme for toll loans, which the Christian People's Party and the Left have, interestingly enough, chosen to be outside. The working party is very sceptical about the government's proposal for the interest compensation scheme. Unfortunately, it is just another and far more bureaucratic method of granting money. But no further path will be built for the means that the government grants to this scheme. In fact, the interest-rate compensation scheme implies that there will be less total funding for the construction of new and important roads for the community, at least if the government is to abide by the rule of action, which they carry out in the notification. Summary: The Labour Party has many thoughts about improving the road sector, but not as the rapporteur describes the Workers' Party's thoughts about what is happening on our roads. We want to organise the road sector in such a way that the community gets the most out of the money. We say no to solutions that cost and create more administration. The working party says yes to new and smarter models to build a road, so that we get more efficient road construction and more money. We say no to OPS as financing solution. We in the Workers' Party will strengthen the State Road Service, which already has competence and experience in implementing large-scale road development, and we will ensure that the State Road Service can manage portfolio development of national road projects through specific priority projects, for example, if it continues as a budgetary option in the future. We say no to establishing a road company, to shared responsibility, new borders and increased administration. The working party will have the most effective collection of tolls, so that the drivers get the best possible way for each crown. So we also say no to the government's interest compensation scheme, which means that there will be less money to build a new road, and not what we want: roads across the whole country, which connects our cities, the parts of the country, and connects us to neighbouring countries.
